1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains (Dam i is between Mountain i and i+1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam (i-1) and Dam i each get x liters of water
4. We're given the final water accumulation A_i for each dam
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's define:
- Let r_i be the rain amount for Mountain i (which is even)
- When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam (i-1) and Dam i each get x liters

So for Mountain i receiving 2x_i liters:
- Dam (i-1) gets x_i liters
- Dam i gets x_i liters

## Mathematical Formulation

For each dam i:
- Dam i receives water from Mountain i-1 and Mountain i
- Dam i accumulates: (rain from Mountain i-1) / 2 + (rain from Mountain i) / 2 = A_i

So for dam i:
- (r_{i-1} / 2) + (r_i / 2) = A_i
- r_{i-1} + r_i = 2A_i

This gives us N equations:
- r_N + r_1 = 2A_1
- r_1 + r_2 = 2A_2
- r_2 + r_3 = 2A_3
- ...
- r_{N-1} + r_N = 2A_N

## Solving the System

We have a system of N equations with N variables. Since N is odd, we can solve it systematically.

From the first equation: r_N = 2A_1 - r_1
From the second equation: r_2 = 2A_2 - r_1
From the third equation: r_3 = 2A_3 - r_2 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - r_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + r_1
And so on...

We can see a pattern emerging. Since N is odd, when we go through all equations and return to r_1, we should get a consistent value.

Let me derive the pattern:
- r_1 = r_1
- r_2 = 2A_2 - r_1
- r_3 = 2A_3 - r_2 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - r_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + r_1
- r_4 = 2A_4 - r_3 = 2A_4 - (2A_3 - 2A_2 + r_1) = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - r_1
- r_5 = 2A_5 - r_4 = 2A_5 - (2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - r_1) = 2A_5 - 2A_4 + 2A_3 - 2A_2 + r_1

The pattern shows that for odd positions, we add r_1, and for even positions, we subtract r_1.

The general formula for the sum of all equations:
Sum of all "r_i + r_{i+1}" = 2 * sum of A_i
But each r_i appears exactly twice (once with r_{i-1} and once with r_i), so:
2 * (r_1 + r_2 + ... + r_N) = 2 * (A_1 + A_2 + ... + A_N)
Therefore: sum(r_i) = sum(A_i)

Wait, let me reconsider the pattern more carefully.

From the equation: r_{i-1} + r_i = 2A_i

For odd N, when we solve the system, we get:
r_i = (-1)^{i+1} * r_1 + some constant term

Actually, let me solve it by substitution:

We can express all r_i in terms of r_1:
- r_2 = 2A_2 - r_1
- r_3 = 2A_3 - r_2 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - r_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + r_1
- r_4 = 2A_4 - r_3 = 2A_4 - (2A_3 - 2A_2 + r_1) = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - r_1
- r_5 = 2A_5 - r_4 = 2A_5 - (2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - r_1) = 2A_5 - 2A_4 + 2A_3 - 2A_2 + r_1

The pattern is: for odd i, coefficient of r_1 is +1, for even i, coefficient is -1.

When we reach the last equation:
r_N + r_1 = 2A_1

Substituting r_N:
- If N is odd, we get:
r_N = (-1)^{N+1} * r_1 + sum of alternating terms
Since N is odd, N+1 is even, so (-1)^{N+1} = 1

So: r_N = (-1)^{N+1} * r_1 + term = r_1 + term

From the pattern, r_N = r_1 + (some alternating sum)

Wait, let me be more systematic. Since N is odd, let's assume:
r_i = (-1)^{i+1} * r_1 + C_i

For i=1: r_1 = (+1) * r_1 + C_1, so C_1 = 0
For i=2: r_2 = (-1) * r_1 + C_2, so C_2 = 2A_2
For i=3: r_3 = (+1) * r_1 + C_3, so C_3 = 2A_3 - 2A_2
For i=4: r_4 = (-1) * r_1 + C_4, so C_4 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2
For i=5: r_5 = (+1) * r_1 + C_5, so C_5 = 2A_5 - 2A_4 + 2A_3 - 2A_2

So in general: C_i = 2 * sum_{j=max(1,1)}^{i} (-1)^{i-j} * A_j

Actually: C_i = 2 * sum_{k=1}^{i} (-1)^{i-k} * A_k

Now plug into the final equation:
r_N + r_1 = 2A_1
But r_N = (-1)^{N+1} * r_1 + C_N
Since N is odd, N+1 is even, so (-1)^{N+1} = 1
So r_N = r_1 + C_N

Thus: r_1 + C_N + r_1 = 2A_1
2r_1 + C_N = 2A_1
r_1 = (2A_1 - C_N) / 2

Now C_N = 2 * sum_{k=1}^{N} (-1)^{N-k} * A_k
So (2A_1 - C_N)/2 = A_1 - sum_{k=1}^{N} (-1)^{N-k} * A_k

Since N is odd, (-1)^{N-k} = (-1)^{odd-k} = (-1)^{1-k} = (-1)^{k-1}

Wait, let me simplify this:
(-1)^{N-k} where N is odd = (-1)^{odd-k} = (-1)^{k-1}

So C_N = 2 * sum_{k=1}^{N} (-1)^{k-1} * A_k

Therefore: r_1 = A_1 - sum_{k=1}^{N} (-1)^{k-1} * A_k

This gives us r_1, and we can compute all other r_i using the pattern.

Actually, let me verify with the sample:
N=3, A=[2,2,4]

r_1 = A_1 - (A_1*(-1)^0 + A_2*(-1)^1 + A_3*(-1)^2)
r_1 = 2 - (2*1 + 2*(-1) + 4*1) = 2 - (2 - 2 + 4) = 2 - 4 = -2

That can't be right since we need non-negative rain.

Let me reconsider the alternating sum more carefully.

For odd N, from the pattern:
r_1 = (2A_1 - C_N) / 2
C_N = sum of terms with alternating signs

From my manual calculation with N=3:
r_1 = r_1
r_2 = 2A_2 - r_1 = 4 - r_1
r_3 = 2A_3 - r_2 = 8 - (4 - r_1) = 4 + r_1
Now r_3 + r_1 = 2A_1 = 4
(4 + r_1) + r_1 = 4
4 + 2r_1 = 4
2r_1 = 0
r_1 = 0

Then r_2 = 4, r_3 = 4.

But the expected output is 4, 0, 4.

Hmm, let me check the dam calculations:
- Dam 1: r_3/2 + r_1/2 = 4/2 + 0/2 = 2 ✓
- Dam 2: r_1/2 + r_2/2 = 0/2 + 4/2 = 2 ✓  
- Dam 3: r_2/2 + r_3/2 = 4/2 + 4/2 = 4 ✓

So the answer should be 4, 0, 4.

I see the issue. In my indexing, when I said "r_i = (-1)^{i+1} * r_1 + C_i", I need to be more careful.

Let me re-derive systematically:

From the equations:
1. r_1 + r_2 = 2A_1
2. r_2 + r_3 = 2A_2  
3. r_3 + r_4 = 2A_3
...
N-1. r_{N-1} + r_N = 2A_{N-1}
N. r_N + r_1 = 2A_N

Solving:
r_2 = 2A_1 - r_1
r_3 = 2A_2 - r_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - r_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + r_1
r_4 = 2A_3 - r_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + r_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - r_1
r_5 = 2A_4 - r_4 = 2A_4 - (2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - r_1) = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + r_1

So the pattern is: r_i = (-1)^{i-1} * r_1 + 2 * sum_{k=1}^{i-1} (-1)^{i-1-k} * A_k

For position i, coefficient of r_1 is (-1)^{i-1}.

Now for the last equation:
r_N + r_1 = 2A_N

Substitute r_N:
r_N = (-1)^{N-1} * r_1 + 2 * sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-1-k} * A_k

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^{N-1} = 1.

So:
r_N = r_1 + 2 * sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-1-k} * A_k

Then:
r_1 + r_N = r_1 + (r_1 + 2 * sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-1-k} * A_k) = 2A_N

So:
2r_1 + 2 * sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-1-k} * A_k = 2A_N

Divide by 2:
r_1 + sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-1-k} * A_k = A_N

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^{N-1-k} = (-1)^{even-k} = (-1)^{-k} = (-1)^k

Wait: (-1)^{even-k} = (-1)^{k} since even-k ≡ -k (mod 2)

So:
r_1 + sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^k * A_k = A_N

Therefore:
r_1 = A_N - sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^k * A_k

For the sample N=3, A=[2,2,4]:
r_1 = 4 - [(-1)^1 * 2 + (-1)^2 * 2] = 4 - [-2 + 2] = 4 - 0 = 4

Then:
r_2 = 2A_1 - r_1 = 4 - 4 = 0
r_3 = 2A_2 - r_2 = 4 - 0 = 4

So the answer is [4, 0, 4] ✓

Perfect! This matches.

## Algorithm

1. Read N and array A of size N
2. Calculate r_1 = A_N - sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^k * A_k
3. For i from 1 to N-1: r_{i+1} = 2A_i - r_i
4. r_N is already computed via the pattern or as the last element
5. Actually, we can compute all values using the recurrence

More precisely:
- r_1 = A_N - sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^k * A_k
- For i from 2 to N: r_i = 2A_{i-1} - r_{i-1}

But note: in our indexing, A_1 to A_N, and mountains 1 to N.

Actually, the first dam equation is r_1 + r_2 = 2A_1, so:
- r_2 = 2A_1 - r_1
- r_3 = 2A_2 - r_2
- ...
- r_N = 2A_{N-1} - r_{N-1}

Then we verify r_N + r_1 = 2A_N to ensure consistency, but the problem states it's always valid.

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate r_1 using the derived formula
# r_1 = A_N - sum_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^k * A_k
# In 0-indexed: r[0] = a[n-1] - sum_{k=0}^{n-2} (-1)^k * a[k]

r = [0] * n

# Calculate r[0]
r_0 = a[n-1]
for k in range(n-1):
    if k % 2 == 0:
        r_0 -= a[k]
    else:
        r_0 += a[k]
r[0] = r_0

# Calculate remaining values using recurrence
for i in range(1, n):
    r[i] = 2 * a[i-1] - r[i-1]

print(' '.join(map(str, r)))
```
